# Assets and resources

The asset settings: [`asset::RegisterAssetSettings`](#asset-registerassetsettings-settings-registry-star) declares the byte budgets of the load buffer pool and of the memory backend and the animation error bounds; the readers return them.

[`asset::AsyncLoader`](#asset-asyncloader): loading without stalling the frame. A request reads its blob and the blobs of its closure on the scheduler's workers (into the manager's buffer pool); the owner thread's `Update` then builds the objects, GPU uploads included, within a time and byte budget, so a frame's cost is bounded. At most `assets.max_inflight` requests read at once, the others wait by priority, and past `assets.max_queued` a request fails with `LimitExceeded`. Two requests for one resource share one load. A request can be cancelled at any stage.

The objects of a closure are built together, as in [`asset::ResourceManager::Load`](#asset-resourcemanager-load-resourceid-handle-lt-t-gt-star-loadfailure-star): a level and everything it names appear at once, while the reading reports its progress.

The audio clip resource: decoded interleaved samples with a rate, a channel count and loop points, minted offline from a WAV file. [`asset::BuildAudioClipBlob`](#asset-buildaudioclipblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-audioclipheader-amp-bytespan-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-const-audioclipprovenance-star) writes an `ACLP` blob, [`asset::AudioClipView`](#asset-audioclipview) reads one in place (the audio module makes its clip from the view without copying `f32` samples).

Layout of the blob's sections (little-endian):

| Section | Content | |---|---| | 16 header | 32 bytes: sample rate `u32`, channels `u32` (1, 2, 4, 6 or 8), stored format `u32` (0 = `f32`, 1 = `s16`), frames `u32`, loop start `u32`, loop end `u32` (0 = no loop), codec `u32` (0 = PCM, 1 = Ogg Vorbis), reserved `u32` (zero) | | 17 samples | PCM: `frames * channels` samples of the stored format, interleaved; Vorbis: the bytes of the Ogg stream as they are (`frames`, `channels` and the rate state what the stream decodes to); 64-byte aligned like every section | 18 provenance | optional, 16 bytes: decoder version `u32` (`0x010307` = libvorbis 1.3.7), reserved `u32`, decode hash `u64` of the `f32` samples; written when a PCM clip was minted from an Ogg stream, so a decoder change shows as a different hash on re-mint |

The asset module knows nothing about conversions: the minter encodes the samples, the audio module converts a `s16` clip to `f32` once, when it makes its clip.

[`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend): the seam every source of resources implements (loose files, the memory backend of a host application, later paks and the network), with [`asset::ResourceReader`](#asset-resourcereader) (ranged reads of one resource) and [`asset::TableTicket`](#asset-tableticket) (the asynchronous read of a backend's id <-> path table).

The versioned blob container every minted resource is stored in: [`asset::BlobWriter`](#asset-blobwriter) writes it, [`asset::BlobReader`](#asset-blobreader) validates and reads it.

Layout (all integers little-endian, no padding other than the alignment gaps, which are zero):

| offset | size | field | |---|---|---| | 0 | 4 | magic `ABLB` | | 4 | 4 | container version ([`asset::kContainerVersion`](#asset-kcontainerversion)) | | 8 | 4 | resource type tag | | 12 | 4 | type version (the registry's current version, nothing else is read) | | 16 | 8 | the blob's own [`asset::ResourceId`](#asset-resourceid) (null only for a `NAMES` blob) | | 24 | 4 | target tag (the platform family the layout was made for; 0 is the development target) | | 28 | 4 | flags (0) | | 32 | 4 | section count | | 36 | 4 | reserved (0) | | 40 | 8 | total size of the blob in bytes | | 48 | 16 | content hash of the whole blob with these 16 bytes zero | | 64 | 24 each | section table: id (u32), reserved (u32, 0), offset (u64), size (u64), ids strictly increasing |

Every section starts at a multiple of 64 bytes (of 4096 when it is 1 MiB or larger) so a loader can hand a span of the file buffer straight to the GPU upload. Section 1 is the standard dependency section (a `u32` count, then `(id u64, type u32, type version u32, role u32)` records strictly sorted by id); ids below 16 are reserved.

[`asset::BlobCache`](#asset-blobcache): a directory of blobs named by their content hash, with an index of which resource id had which hash last, bounded in bytes with least-recently-used eviction. The network backend keeps the small blobs it fetched in it, so they are not fetched twice and are served when the server cannot be reached.

A file is written to a temporary and renamed over its name (`io::WriteFileAtomic`), so a crash leaves a whole file or none. Opening the cache hashes every file and drops the ones that do not match their name (a damaged or foreign file), and files that no index entry names. A read of a cached blob does not hash it again: the cache is local and was verified when it was opened and when the blob was put.

Block compression for the texture minter: decoders for BC1, BC2, BC3, BC4, BC5 and BC7, and encoders for BC1, BC4, BC5 and BC7, in exact integer arithmetic so the output is byte-identical on every compiler, machine and worker count.

A block is 4x4 texels, row-major, stored in 8 bytes (BC1, BC4) or 16 bytes (BC2, BC3, BC5, BC7). The decoders give 8-bit RGBA texels (BC4 puts its channel in red and BC5 its two in red and green; the others read 0 and alpha 255). The interpolated colors of BC1 are `(2a + b) / 3` and `(a + b) / 2` of the 8-bit expanded endpoints, truncated: the decoder is the reference of this engine. A GPU's BC1, BC4 and BC5 decode can differ from it by up to two steps per channel (a Radeon 860M on D3D12 and Vulkan, `tests/rhi/bc_gpu_test.cpp`; rounding the palette instead does not bring it to one); BC7, specified in integers, matches exactly. The encoders choose among endpoint sets by the sum of squared channel errors and break every tie in favor of the lower index or the first candidate tried, so the result depends on the input texels only. A partial block at the right or bottom edge repeats its last column and row.

The animation clip blob (type `ANIM`, version 1): per-joint keyframe tracks and events for one skeleton, built by [`asset::BuildClipBlob`](#asset-buildclipblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-clipbuildinput-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-f32-star-f32-star) and read in place through [`asset::ClipView`](#asset-clipview).

Sections (little-endian, ids from 16): 16 header (96 bytes: duration, sample rate hint, flags, joint count, track count, event count, the skeleton hash, the measured error bounds, the bound box), 17 the track table (72 bytes per animated joint), 18 the key data, 19 the events (16 bytes each). The skeleton is the blob's one dependency (role [`asset::kClipRoleSkeleton`](#asset-kcliproleskeleton)).

A channel (translation, rotation or scale) of a track is either constant (its value sits in the track table, no keys) or keyed. Keyed times are `u16` ticks: 1 / sample rate when every key lies on that grid, otherwise duration / 65535. Rotations are smallest-three: the largest component is dropped (made positive), the other three stored as `SNorm16`, the first of them as 14 bits with the dropped index in the low two bits of its word (6 bytes per key). Translations and scales are `UNorm16x3` between a per-channel minimum and extent stored with the channel. Sampling interpolates linearly (rotations: normalized shortest-arc lerp).

[`asset::ContentHash`](#asset-contenthash): the 128-bit content hash of a blob (SHA-256 truncated to its first 16 bytes, the choice of a measured comparison). It names blobs in the network cache, lets `verify` and the packer detect corruption and identifies a base pak. It is not on the load path of a local file, and it is not a security feature (the engine's networking is for trusted networks).

The loaders of the resource types that need no GPU, and the traits that tie their C++ types to resource types. The GPU-backed loaders for meshes, textures and materials are registered over these.

Cube map geometry for the minters: face directions and the equirectangular-to-cube conversion.

The conversion uses only `+ - * /` and the square root, with the angle functions replaced by fixed polynomials, so the bytes are the same on every machine.

[`asset::FaultyBackend`](#asset-faultybackend): a wrapper around any [`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend) that injects failures, chunked reads and latency, for the tests of every later day (the file layer has a network-like double for the same job).

[`asset::FilesystemBackend`](#asset-filesystembackend): a directory of loose blobs served through [`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend), the backend of development builds.

The loaders of the resource types that live on the GPU: meshes, textures and materials. [`asset::RegisterGpuLoaders`](#asset-registergpuloaders-resourcemanager-star-gpuassetcontext-star) registers them over the CPU loaders, so the same ids load as objects that own device resources, made recreatable: a device reset rebuilds them from the manager's own bytes.

The height map resource: 16-bit terrain heights over a height range, their mip chain, and a pyramid of block minima and maxima. [`asset::BuildHeightMapBlob`](#asset-buildheightmapblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-heightmapheader-amp-bytespan-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) writes an `HGHT` blob, [`asset::HeightMapView`](#asset-heightmapview) reads one in place (the renderer makes its height texture from the levels, the physics module its height field from level 0 and the blocks, without copying).

Layout of the blob's sections (little-endian):

| Section | Content | |---|---| | 16 header | 32 bytes: width `u32`, height `u32` (samples, 2 to 8192), mip count `u32` (the full chain), block cells `u32` (8), range minimum `f32`, range maximum `f32`, cell size `f32` (metres between samples), reserved `u32` (zero) | | 17 heights | the levels packed from level 0, each `width * height` `u16` samples in rows; a level is half the one before (rounded down, at least 1), each sample the rounded mean of the four above it (the edge repeated); 64-byte aligned | | 18 blocks | for each block of 8 by 8 cells of level 0 (the last ones smaller), row by row: minimum `u16` then maximum `u16` of the samples the block touches, its edges included |

A sample `s` is the height `range_min + s / 65535 * (range_max - range_min)`.

The convex hull resource: the vertices, the face polygons with their planes and the mass data of a convex hull, for the physics module's collision shape. [`asset::BuildHullGeometry`](#asset-buildhullgeometry-span-lt-const-array-lt-f32-3-gt-gt-hullgeometry-star) computes the hull of a point cloud, [`asset::BuildHullBlob`](#asset-buildhullblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-hullgeometry-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) writes a `HULL` blob, [`asset::HullView`](#asset-hullview) reads one in place.

Layout of the blob's sections (little-endian, one section each):

| Section | Content | |---|---| | 16 header | 80 bytes: vertex count `u32`, face count `u32`, index count `u32`, flags `u32` (zero), volume `f32`, centroid `f32 x3`, inertia `f32 x6` (xx, yy, zz, xy, xz, yz of the tensor about the centroid at unit density), extent `f32 x3` (the largest absolute coordinate on each axis), 12 zero bytes | | 17 vertices | `f32 x3` per vertex | | 18 planes | `f32 x4` per face: the outward unit normal and `d`, with `dot(n, p) == d` on the face | | 19 faces | two `u32` per face: the first index and the number of vertices | | 20 indices | `u16` per index: the vertices of each face, counter-clockwise seen from outside |

The level blob (type `LEVL`, version 1): entities as the storage module's records, the table of every resource they name, and the list of levels it includes. Built by [`asset::BuildLevelBlob`](#asset-buildlevelblob-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-resourceid-const-leveldata-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-const-storage-codeclimits-amp), read by [`asset::ParseLevelBlob`](#asset-parselevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-leveldata-star-resourceid-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp) and put into a world by [`asset::LoadLevelBlob`](#asset-loadlevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-ecs-world-amp-storage-keyindex-amp-leveldata-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp).

A level is the same data a save is: one [`storage::EntityRecord`](storage.md#storage-entityrecord) per entity (the entity container of `storage::WorldStorage`: a count, then framed component records encoded by the [`storage::ComponentRegistry`](storage.md#storage-componentregistry)), so there is one set of field codecs for saves and levels. What a level adds is what a save has no use for: the references. Every field declared with [`storage::FieldOptions::Reference`](storage.md#storage-fieldoptions-reference-u32-bool) (or typed [`storage::ResourceRef`](storage.md#storage-resourceref)) names a resource by id; the blob lists each such use in a table and each distinct resource as a dependency, so the manager can load the closure before a single entity exists.

Sections (ids from [`asset::kFirstUserSection`](#asset-kfirstusersection)): 16 the header (64 bytes: the entity, record, reference and include counts), 17 the entity table (32 bytes per entity: key high, key low, offset into section 18, size, record count; keys strictly increasing, the offsets tiling section 18), 18 the entity containers one after another, 19 the reference table (24 bytes each: entity index, component id, field index, type tag, id; in entity, record and field order; null references are not listed). The standard dependency section lists every distinct referenced resource and every included level (role bits [`asset::kLevelRoleReference`](#asset-klevelrolereference) and [`asset::kLevelRoleInclude`](#asset-klevelroleinclude)).

A stored component is read only at its registered version; a level written with an older one is refused with `UnsupportedVersion` and fixed by re-minting from its source, never converted.

[`asset::LoadBufferPool`](#asset-loadbufferpool): reusable, pre-faulted buffers that resources are read into. A measurement found a read into a fresh buffer at four times the cost of a read into one whose pages were already touched, so the pool touches the pages once, when it creates a buffer, and keeps the buffer for the next load of that size.

The material blob (type `MATL`, version 1): a template name, shader permutation options, the parameter block in the template's uniform layout, texture bindings by resource id, sampler descriptions and render-state overrides. Built by [`asset::BuildMaterialBlob`](#asset-buildmaterialblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-materialassetdata-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) and read by [`asset::LoadMaterialBlob`](#asset-loadmaterialblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-materialassetdata-star-resourceid-star).

Sections (ids from [`asset::kFirstUserSection`](#asset-kfirstusersection)): 16 the header (96 bytes: flags, counts, the state overrides and the 48-byte template name), 17 the parameter block (the bytes copied into the material's uniform buffer, laid out as the template's block, so loading copies one block), 18 the parameter table (name, type, offset and size of each value the source set; 64 bytes each), 19 the texture bindings (slot name and resource id; 64 bytes each), 20 the samplers (64 bytes each), 21 the permutation options (48-byte names). Every texture id is also a dependency of the blob (type `TEXR`, role = the binding's index), so the loader follows the references without reading the sections.

[`asset::HostMemoryBackend`](#asset-hostmemorybackend): a real backend for resources whose bytes the host application provides at run time (the content a host that embeds the engine registers through the C API), with no file, pak or server behind it.

The mesh blob (type `MESH`, version 1): geometry laid out for the GPU, built by [`asset::BuildMeshBlob`](#asset-buildmeshblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-meshbuildinput-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) and read zero-copy through [`asset::MeshView`](#asset-meshview).

Sections (ids from 16, little-endian, each 64-byte aligned by the container): 16 header (96 bytes), 17 attributes (16 bytes each: location, format, offset, binding), 18 vertex stream 0 (the exact bytes of the vertex buffer), 19 indices (16 or 32 bit, a triangle list), 20 LODs (16 bytes each), 21 material slots (40 bytes each), and for a skinned mesh 22 the skin stream (binding 1: `UInt8x4` joint indices at location 5, `UNorm8x4` weights at location 6, summing to exactly 255 per vertex) and 23 the bind-pose box of each joint's influence set. A skinned mesh declares its skeleton as a dependency (role [`asset::kMeshRoleSkinSkeleton`](#asset-kmeshroleskinskeleton)) and records the skeleton hash and joint count. Morph targets are sections 40 (the targets, 48 bytes each: a name of up to 31 bytes, the first delta, the delta count) and 41 (the deltas, 40 bytes each: the vertex, then position, normal and tangent deltas as `f32x3`; a target's deltas sorted by vertex, no repeats). An older reader refused section 40, so a blob with targets is refused there and one without is unchanged: the version stays 1. The box holds every target at weights from -1 to 1.

Per-vertex colours are one more attribute of stream 0, `UNorm8x4` at location 4 after the others (offset 24 packed, 48 full): rgb sRGB-encoded, alpha linear, as `gbuffer.vert`'s `VERTEX_COLOR` reads them. The version-1 reader already took any attribute, so a coloured mesh is still version 1.

The minted triangle mesh of a static physics collider (`MCOL`): positions, triangle indices and a material index per triangle. The bounding-volume hierarchy is built by the physics module when the mesh is added (deterministically, from these bytes). The reader treats the bytes as hostile.

[`asset::MountTable`](#asset-mounttable): the ordered set of backends the resource manager reads from. A lookup by id goes to the highest-priority backend that has the resource (so a loose-file mount overrides a pak), and the mounts' id <-> path tables are read asynchronously and merged.

[`asset::NetworkBackend`](#asset-networkbackend): a [`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend) whose resources live on a [`asset::ResourceServer`](#asset-resourceserver). It talks the protocol of `resource_protocol.h` over a [`net::Transport`](net.md#net-transport) it is given (the TCP transport over sockets, or over the simulated streams in tests), keeps the blobs it fetched in a [`asset::BlobCache`](#asset-blobcache) so a blob is not fetched twice and is served when the server cannot be reached, retries a failed request with a bounded exponential back-off, and reports `Offline` when it gives up, reconnecting in the background.

**Reading.** A resource of at most `assets.net.prefetch_bytes` is fetched whole (and verified against the hash its header records, then cached), larger ones are read by range, a request each. Before it fetches a small blob it reads its header and asks the cache whether the hash is held: the cache is keyed by content, so a blob that changed on the server is fetched and one that did not is not.

**Offline.** When the retries run out the state is `Offline`: `Stat` and `Open` are served from the cache for an id the cache knows (the hash it last saw, with the blob held), the table is the last one read, and everything else returns `Offline`. A background reconnect (every `reconnect_nanos`) brings it back `Online`. The resource manager treats `Offline` like `NotFound` and does not remember the failure.

**Driving.** The transport is polled by a thread of the backend's own by default. A test (or a program with its own loop) can turn that off and call [`asset::NetworkBackend::Poll`](#asset-networkbackend-poll); the blocking calls then poll while they wait and call the `idle` function between polls (a test moves its clock and updates its server there).

The pak: many resources in one file, found by id ([`asset::PakWriter`](#asset-pakwriter), [`asset::PakReader`](#asset-pakreader)).

All integers are little-endian. The layout, in file order:

| Part | Size | Content | |---|---|---| | header | 128 | magic `APAK`, format version, flags, target tag, alignment, type count, entry count, type table offset, names offset and size, base pak identity (16 bytes, zero for none), reserved zero | | type table | 8 each | `(tag u32, version u32)` for each resource type the pak holds, sorted by tag | | names | any | the id <-> path table as a `NAMES` blob (optional, flag bit 0), 64-byte aligned | | entries | any | the blobs in id order, each 64-byte aligned (4096 from 1 MiB), zero padding between | | index | 48 each | `(id u64, offset u64, size u64, content hash 16, type u32, reserved u32)` sorted by id | | footer | 48 | index offset, entry count, index hash (16), file size, magic `KPAK`, format version |

The pak's identity (what a patch pak names as its base) is its index hash. Version 1 stores blobs uncompressed.

[`asset::PakBackend`](#asset-pakbackend): a pak file served through [`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend) (read-only).

[`asset::ResourceId`](#asset-resourceid): a resource's stable identity, and the rules for the virtual paths that name it. An id is a permanent 64-bit number assigned once by `asset_mint adopt`; it is not derived from the path, so a rename or a move never changes it. The runtime refers to resources by id only; a path is an alias for tools and sources.

[`asset::ResourceManager`](#asset-resourcemanager): ask for a resource by id, get a handle, never know which backend served it.

The manager owns the mount table ([`asset::MountTable`](#asset-mounttable)), the pool the blobs are read into ([`asset::LoadBufferPool`](#asset-loadbufferpool)) and a cache of loaded resources. `Load<T>` reads the blob into one aligned buffer, checks its container and version, loads its **whole closure first** (only the dependencies the blob declares, each of the type and version the declaration says) and then calls the loader registered for its type; the handle is published only when all of that succeeded, so a resource is never half loaded and a dependency can never be broken. A failure returns the status and no handle (there is no silent fallback; see [`asset::ResourceManager::RegisterFallback`](#asset-resourcemanager-registerfallback-resourcetype-bytespan)).

A handle is an owning, reference-counted reference with a generation: a copy adds one, the last one frees the resource (unless it is pinned), and a handle whose resource was unloaded reads as invalid, never as another resource. A loaded resource holds a reference on each of its dependencies, so a dependency cannot go while a dependent is loaded.

The resource protocol, version 1: the messages a resource client and a [`asset::ResourceServer`](#asset-resourceserver) exchange over a [`net::Transport`](net.md#net-transport). Every message is one transport message, encoded with [`net::NetWriter`](net.md#net-netwriter) (and so decoded with [`net::NetReader`](net.md#net-netreader), which refuses a second encoding of one message). A message starts with its type and a request id the response repeats, so responses may interleave.

| Message | Direction | Fields after the type and the request id | |---|---|---| | `Hello` | client to server | protocol version (u16), the largest response the client takes (u32) | | `HelloAck` | server to client | version (u16), largest read (u32), entries per page (u32), requests per poll (u32) | | `Stat` / `StatOk` | both | id (u64) / size in bytes (u64) | | `Table` | client to server | the generation the client has (u64, 0 for none), page (u32) | | `TableOk` | server to client | generation (u64), unchanged (bool), page (u32), page count (u32), then unless unchanged: a count and per entry the id (u64), the type tag (u32) and the path (string) | | `List` / `ListOk` | both | prefix (string), page (u32) / generation (u64), page (u32), more (bool), a count and the ids (u64) | | `Read` / `ReadOk` | both | id (u64), offset (u64), length (u32) / offset (u64), the bytes | | `Error` | server to client | a code ([`asset::ProtoError`](#asset-protoerror)) |

**Limits.** A path or prefix is at most [`asset::kProtoMaxPathBytes`](#asset-kprotomaxpathbytes) bytes, a page at most [`asset::kProtoMaxPageEntries`](#asset-kprotomaxpageentries) entries, a read at most the server's `assets.net.max_read_bytes`; every length is checked against its limit and the bytes that remain before anything is allocated. The protocol has no request that writes: a server is read-only. A server closes a connection that sends a message it cannot decode, one before `Hello`, or a second `Hello`; a request it cannot serve is answered with an `Error`.

[`asset::ResourceServer`](#asset-resourceserver): serves any [`asset::ResourceBackend`](#asset-resourcebackend) (a directory, a pak, host memory) to many clients over a [`net::Transport`](net.md#net-transport), speaking the protocol of `resource_protocol.h`. It is read-only (the protocol has no request that writes), bounded (a read, a page, the requests per connection and per poll have limits the settings give) and polled: [`asset::ResourceServer::Update`](#asset-resourceserver-update) takes the transport's events, answers the requests and returns. The transport's own limits (connections, rate limits, queues) apply as they are. There is no encryption: use it on a network you trust.

[`asset::ResourceTable`](#asset-resourcetable): the id <-> path map of the engine (the user's decision of 2026-11-13: one table where the map lives). It is a value type: each backend supplies its own (a stored `NAMES` blob, a pak section, a server's reply, or one built on demand by scanning a folder), and the mount table merges them by priority. The load path never uses it: the runtime refers to resources by id, and paths are resolved at the edges.

[`asset::ResourceType`](#asset-resourcetype) (a four-character tag) and [`asset::TypeRegistry`](#asset-typeregistry) (the known types and the one current version of each, the single source of truth the minters, the packer, `verify` and the manager share).

The script resource: the source text of a script with the facts the minter found in it, so a load never parses to learn them. [`asset::BuildScriptBlob`](#asset-buildscriptblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-scriptbuild-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) writes an `SCRP` blob, [`asset::ScriptView`](#asset-scriptview) reads one in place (the source is a range of the loaded buffer, shared, never copied).

Layout of the blob's sections (little-endian). The dependencies are the container's standard dependency section (other scripts the source requires, by id, type `SCRP`).

| Section | Content | |---|---| | 16 header | 32 bytes: source bytes `u32`, entry count `u32`, source hash `u64` (first 8 bytes of the content hash of the source), language name bytes `u32`, language version bytes `u32`, reserved `u64` (zero) | | 17 source | UTF-8 text, line endings normalized to `\n`, as validated by the minter | | 18 language | the language name then its version text (lengths in the header) | | 19 entry points | per entry: name length `u32`, the name; the behaviors the validator found the source defines | | 20 bytecode | optional: key length `u32`, the key text, then the precompiled chunk; used only when the loader's key is equal, never required |

The asset module knows nothing about languages: the minter validates and names, the script module loads.

The skeleton resource: the joint hierarchy, the bind pose and the inverse bind matrices that skinned meshes and animation clips refer to. [`asset::BuildSkeletonBlob`](#asset-buildskeletonblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-span-lt-const-skeletonjoint-gt-bool-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) writes a `SKEL` blob, [`asset::SkeletonView`](#asset-skeletonview) reads one in place.

Layout of the blob's sections (all little-endian, one section each, 64-byte aligned by the container):

| Section | Content | |---|---| | 16 header | 48 bytes: joint count `u32`, flags `u32` (bit 0: names present), skeleton hash (16 bytes), bind-pose bound center `f32 x3`, radius `f32`, two zero `u32` | | 17 parents | `u16` per joint; 0xFFFF for a root; a parent index is always below the joint's own | | 18 translations | `f32 x3` per joint: the bind pose, local space | | 19 rotations | `f32 x4` (x, y, z, w) per joint: unit quaternions with w >= 0 | | 20 scales | `f32 x3` per joint | | 21 inverse bind | 3x4 row-major `f32` (48 bytes) per joint: the bytes the GPU joint palette takes | | 22 name hashes | `u32` per joint (FNV-1a of the name; only with names) | | 23 names | `u32` offsets (joint count + 1) then the characters, no terminators (only with names) |

Errors of the asset pipeline, reported as values: [`asset::ErrorCode`](#asset-errorcode) and [`asset::Status`](#asset-status). No asset failure aborts, and no input, however damaged, is a reason to abort.

The texture blob (type `TEXR`, version 1): every mip level of every layer, laid out the way the upload wants, built by [`asset::BuildTextureBlob`](#asset-buildtextureblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-texturebuildinput-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32) and read zero-copy through [`asset::TextureView`](#asset-textureview).

Sections (ids from 16, little-endian, each 64-byte aligned by the container): 16 header (64 bytes), 17 the level table (16 bytes per subresource: `u64` offset into the data section, `u32` size, `u32` row pitch; subresource `layer * mip_count + mip`), 18 the data. There is no "generate at load" flag: every level is stored, and the mip filter, the sRGB handling and the premultiplication happened in the minter. Each subresource starts at a multiple of the recorded subresource alignment (an offset in the data section, which the container aligns to 64 bytes) and its rows are `row_pitch` bytes apart (the row size rounded up to the row alignment and the texel size), so the loader hands the spans to the upload unchanged. The alignments depend on the target API (`TextureLayoutForTarget`); `RetargetTextureBlob` re-lays a blob out for another target.

Minting a texture blob from decoded pixels.

The core of the texture minter, in the engine so that `asset_mint` (from `.tex.src` and raw images) and the glTF importer (from decoded PNG and JPEG) make identical blobs: 8-bit RGBA layers are premultiplied if asked, converted to the storage format, filtered into a mip chain in exact integer arithmetic and, for the BC formats, block-compressed, all deterministically.

Mip generation for the texture minter: a deterministic filter in exact integer arithmetic, so the same input gives byte-identical output on every compiler, machine and thread count.

The formats are `R8_UNorm`, `RG8_UNorm`, `RGBA8_UNorm`, `RGBA8_SRGB`, `R16_Float`, `RGBA16_Float`. An sRGB texture is filtered in linear light (a 16-bit linear value per channel, through a fixed table). Half-float texels are exact multiples of 2^-24 and are filtered as such integers. Nothing here uses floating-point arithmetic.

A level of `w x h` texels gives a next level of `max(1, w / 2) x max(1, h / 2)`. Destination texel `i` of an axis (source length `s`, destination length `d`) covers the source range `[i * s / d, (i + 1) * s / d)` (integer division), so an odd length gives its last texel a range of three. The box filter averages that range; the Kaiser filter is the fixed 6-tap kernel `-5 24 109 109 24 -5` (sum 256, a Kaiser-windowed sinc with beta 4) over the source texels `lo - 2 .. lo + 3` (`lo` the start of the range), edges clamped; an axis of length 1 is copied.

## `RegisterManagerSettings(settings::Registry*)` {#asset-registermanagersettings-settings-registry-star}

`function asset::RegisterManagerSettings` — defined in `engine/asset/asset_settings.h`

```cpp
void RegisterManagerSettings(settings::Registry* registry);
```

Declares the settings of the resource manager: `assets.cache_bytes` (the most bytes of resident resources; 0 for no limit), `assets.allow_fallbacks` (a development setting, default off: a missing or invalid resource of a type that has a registered fallback is replaced by it, visibly and counted), `assets.verify_on_load` (default off: check the content hash of every blob when it is loaded) and `assets.mounts` (the mounts: `PREFIX=PATH` entries separated by `;`, a path being a directory of loose blobs or a pak file; the prefix `/` when none is written; later entries win over earlier ones). A key that is already declared is left as it is.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; call it while the registry is being set up. |
| Ownership | borrows its pointer arguments for the call only; nothing is retained. |

A key that is already declared is left as it is.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; call it while the registry is being set up. |
| Ownership | borrows its pointer arguments for the call only; nothing is retained. |

## `LoadTicket` {#asset-loadticket}

`class asset::LoadTicket` — defined in `engine/asset/async_loader.h`

```cpp
class LoadTicket { ... }
```

The caller's end of an asynchronous load: poll it, wait for it through the loader, finish it into a handle, or cancel it.

A ticket that is destroyed or cancelled without being finished stops waiting; when no ticket waits for a load any more, the load is cancelled.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; `IsDone`, `Progress` and `Cancel` are thread-safe. |
| Ownership | shares the load's state with the loader; the loader (and the manager) must outlive the loads in flight. |

### `LoadLevelAsync(ResourceId,LoadPriority,const storage::CancelToken*)` {#asset-asyncloader-loadlevelasync-resourceid-loadpriority-const-storage-canceltoken-star}

`method asset::AsyncLoader::LoadLevelAsync` — defined in `engine/asset/async_loader.h`

```cpp
LoadTicket LoadLevelAsync(ResourceId id, LoadPriority priority = LoadPriority::Normal, const storage::CancelToken* cancel = nullptr);
```

Loads a level as a streamed unit: its blob is read, then the blobs of what it names, those of the levels it includes first and the rest in the order the level lists them, and [`asset::LoadTicket::Progress`](#asset-loadticket-progress-u32-star-u32-star-const) reports how many are read. The level and its dependencies are built together by one `Update`, as `Load` builds a closure, so the level is active when its ticket is done.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `id` | the level. |
| Returns | a ticket of the level's summary ([`asset::LevelSummary`](#asset-levelsummary), whatever loader the manager has for the level type). |
| Ownership | as `LoadAsync`. |

### `Update(u64,u64)` {#asset-asyncloader-update-u64-u64}

`method asset::AsyncLoader::Update` — defined in `engine/asset/async_loader.h`

```cpp
LoadUpdate Update(u64 max_microseconds, u64 max_bytes);
```

Builds the objects of the requests whose blobs were read, most urgent first, until `max_microseconds` of the clock or `max_bytes` of blobs are spent (at least one request is built per call when any waits), and starts queued requests as reading slots free. Call it once per frame on the thread that owns the GPU objects.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `max_microseconds` | the time budget; 0 for none. |
| Parameter `max_bytes` | the byte budget; 0 for none. |
| Returns | what was done. |

### `Wait(const LoadTicket&)` {#asset-asyncloader-wait-const-loadticket-amp}

`method asset::AsyncLoader::Wait` — defined in `engine/asset/async_loader.h`

```cpp
Status Wait(const LoadTicket& ticket);
```

Runs `Update` without a budget (and the scheduler's jobs when it has no workers) until the ticket is done.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | the outcome of the load, as [`asset::LoadTicket::Result`](#asset-loadticket-result-const). |
| Fails | an empty ticket is `Invalid`. |

## `BuildAudioClipBlob(const TypeRegistry&,ResourceId,const AudioClipHeader&,ByteSpan,Vector<byte>*,u32,const AudioClipProvenance*)` {#asset-buildaudioclipblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-audioclipheader-amp-bytespan-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-const-audioclipprovenance-star}

`function asset::BuildAudioClipBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/audio_clip.h`

```cpp
Status BuildAudioClipBlob(const TypeRegistry& types, ResourceId id, const AudioClipHeader& header, ByteSpan samples, Vector<byte>* out, u32 target = 0, const AudioClipProvenance* provenance = nullptr);
```

Writes the blob of an audio clip, deterministically.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `types` | the registry; it must hold `kAudioClip`. |
| Parameter `id` | the clip's resource id. |
| Parameter `header` | the description; every field is checked. |
| Parameter `samples` | PCM: the encoded samples, exactly `frames * channels` samples of `header.storage`; Vorbis: the Ogg bytes (at least one, at most `kMaxAudioClipVorbisBytes`). |
| Parameter `out` | receives the blob bytes. |
| Parameter `target` | the target platform code of the blob header. |
| Parameter `provenance` | written as section 18 when given (PCM clips only); may be null. |
| Fails | `Invalid` naming the first broken rule; the errors of [`asset::BlobWriter::Finish`](#asset-blobwriter-finish-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-const). |
| Ownership | `samples` is read during the call; `out` receives a copy. |

## `BackendCapability` {#asset-backendcapability}

`enum asset::BackendCapability` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`

```cpp
enum BackendCapability : u32 { ... }
```

What a backend can do ([`asset::ResourceBackend::Capabilities`](#asset-resourcebackend-capabilities-const) is a mask of these).

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="asset-kbackendranged"></a>`kBackendRanged` | `1` | `ReadAt` reads any range without reading the rest. |
| <a id="asset-kbackendasync"></a>`kBackendAsync` | `2` | `ReadAtAsync` completes on its own, without a thread of the caller's. |
| <a id="asset-kbackendwatch"></a>`kBackendWatch` | `4` | `Watch` reports changes. |
| <a id="asset-kbackendwritable"></a>`kBackendWritable` | `8` | The backend accepts changes from its host (the memory backend). |
| <a id="asset-kbackendstoredtable"></a>`kBackendStoredTable` | `16` | The backend has a stored id <-> path table (a special file, a pak section, a reply). |
| <a id="asset-kbackendasynctable"></a>`kBackendAsyncTable` | `32` | `ReadTableAsync` reads natively, without a thread (the network backend). |

## `ResourceReader` {#asset-resourcereader}

`class asset::ResourceReader` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`

```cpp
class ResourceReader { ... }
```

An open resource: ranged reads of its bytes.

A reader keeps the bytes it reads valid for its lifetime even when the backend replaces the resource meanwhile.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; `ReadAt` calls on one reader may run concurrently when the backend says so, and at most one `ReadAtAsync` is in flight at a time. A reader must not be destroyed while one is in flight. |
| Ownership | owned by the caller (a [`UniquePtr`](foundation-containers.md#uniqueptr)); it must not outlive the backend that opened it. |

## `TableTicket` {#asset-tableticket}

`class asset::TableTicket` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`

```cpp
class TableTicket { ... }
```

The asynchronous read of a backend's table: started by [`asset::ResourceBackend::ReadTableAsync`](#asset-resourcebackend-readtableasync), finished by `Finish` (the whole table) or consumed page by page with `TakePage`.

The table is complete or an error, never silently partial: a cancelled or failed read yields no table from `Finish` (pages already taken stay with the caller, and the status says why the read did not finish).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe; `Publish` and `Complete` belong to the producer, the rest to the consumer. |
| Ownership | owned by the caller; destroying a ticket cancels the read and waits for it to end, and the backend it was started on must outlive it. |

### `TableTicket()` {#asset-tableticket-tableticket}

`constructor asset::TableTicket::TableTicket` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`

```cpp
TableTicket();
```

Makes a ticket for a read that a producer will fill (see [`asset::TableTicket::RunBlocking`](#asset-tableticket-runblocking-resourcebackend-amp) for the usual way).

### `TableTicket(const TableTicket&)` {#asset-tableticket-tableticket-const-tableticket-amp}

```cpp

```

_Deleted._

`operator asset::TableTicket::operator=` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`


_Deleted._


```cpp
bool SetOnComplete(void (*function)(void* user), void* user);
```

Registers a function called once when the read ends, on the producer's thread. The function may call `Finish`, `TakePage` and `IsDone`, but it must not destroy the ticket (the destructor waits for the function). At most one function; a second call replaces the first.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | false, without registering, when the read already ended (the caller then acts as if called). |
| Ownership | the ticket stores `function` and `user` until the read ends; `user` must stay valid until then. |

`method asset::TableTicket::Publish` — defined in `engine/asset/backend.h`

```cpp

```

Adds a page of complete entries (called by the producer of the read).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | `Cancelled` when the consumer cancelled (the producer then calls `Complete`); `DuplicateId` when an entry clashes with an earlier one. |


Ends the read (called by the producer of the read, exactly once).

A source of resources. See the day-315 plan and `docs/guide/assets.md`.

Every virtual member of this class and of its helpers is defined inline, so a test double in an RTTI-enabled translation unit links against the RTTI-free library.

## `SetBlobTarget(ByteSpan,u32,Vector<byte>*)` {#asset-setblobtarget-bytespan-u32-vector-lt-byte-gt-star}

`function asset::SetBlobTarget` — defined in `engine/asset/blob.h`

```cpp
Status SetBlobTarget(ByteSpan blob, u32 target, Vector<byte>* out);
```

Copies a blob with another target tag in its header and the content hash recomputed (the bytes after the header are the same). The layout of the blob is not changed: a type whose layout depends on the target (a texture) is laid out again by its own function (`RetargetTextureBlob`).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `blob` | the blob. |
| Parameter `target` | the new target tag. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the copy. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` for bytes that are not a blob header; `UnsupportedVersion` for another container version. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | borrows `blob` for the call only; `*out` is replaced. |

## `PeekBlobHeader(ByteSpan,BlobHeaderInfo*)` {#asset-peekblobheader-bytespan-blobheaderinfo-star}

`function asset::PeekBlobHeader` — defined in `engine/asset/blob.h`

```cpp
Status PeekBlobHeader(ByteSpan header, BlobHeaderInfo* out);
```

Reads the header fields of a blob from its first `kBlobHeaderBytes` bytes, so a backend can learn a file's id and type without reading the file.

Checks the magic and the container version only; the type, the type version and the rest are checked by [`asset::BlobReader::Open`](#asset-blobreader-open-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `header` | at least `kBlobHeaderBytes` bytes from the start of the blob. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` when the bytes are shorter than a header or do not start with the blob magic; `UnsupportedVersion` when the container version is not the current one. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | borrows `header` for the call only. |

What the cache last learned about `id`: the content hash of its blob and its size, even when the blob itself is not held (a name known from the last time the server was reached).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | false when nothing is known. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | the outputs are written on success. |

### `Put(ResourceId,const ContentHash&,ByteSpan)` {#asset-blobcache-put-resourceid-const-contenthash-amp-bytespan}

`method asset::BlobCache::Put` — defined in `engine/asset/blob_cache.h`

```cpp
Status Put(ResourceId id, const ContentHash& hash, ByteSpan bytes);
```

Stores a blob under its hash, remembers that `id` has it and evicts the least recently used blobs beyond the limit. A blob larger than the limit is not held.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `hash` | the hash the blob records. |
| Parameter `bytes` | the whole blob. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` when the bytes do not hash to `hash` (nothing is stored); `Io`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `bytes` is read during the call only. |

## `BcKind` {#asset-bckind}

`enum asset::BcKind` — defined in `engine/asset/block_compress.h`

```cpp
enum class BcKind : u32 { ... }
```

A block-compression scheme the decoders know.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc1"></a>`Bc1` | `0` | Color with 1-bit alpha, 8 bytes. |
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc2"></a>`Bc2` | `1` | Color with 4-bit explicit alpha, 16 bytes (decode only). |
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc3"></a>`Bc3` | `2` | Color with interpolated alpha, 16 bytes. |
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc4"></a>`Bc4` | `3` | One channel, 8 bytes. |
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc5"></a>`Bc5` | `4` | Two channels, 16 bytes. |
| <a id="asset-bckind-bc7"></a>`Bc7` | `5` | High-quality color with alpha, 16 bytes (eight modes to decode; the encoder writes mode 6). |

The encoder's effort: more refinement passes find lower errors and cost more time.

| <a id="asset-bcquality-normal"></a>`Normal` | `1` | The range fit and two refinement passes. |
| <a id="asset-bcquality-high"></a>`High` | `2` | The range fit and four refinement passes. |

## `DecodeImage(BcKind,u32,u32,ByteSpan,Vector<byte>*)` {#asset-decodeimage-bckind-u32-u32-bytespan-vector-lt-byte-gt-star}

`function asset::DecodeImage` — defined in `engine/asset/block_compress.h`

```cpp
Status DecodeImage(BcKind kind, u32 width, u32 height, ByteSpan blocks, Vector<byte>* rgba);
```

Decodes a whole image of blocks (rows of `ceil(width / 4)` blocks, tightly packed) into tight RGBA8 texels.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `kind` | the scheme. |
| Parameter `width` | 1 to [`asset::kMaxTextureSize`](#asset-kmaxtexturesize). |
| Parameter `height` | 1 to [`asset::kMaxTextureSize`](#asset-kmaxtexturesize). |
| Parameter `blocks` | exactly `ceil(w / 4) * ceil(h / 4) * BcBlockBytes(kind)` bytes. |
| Parameter `rgba` | replaced by `width * height * 4` bytes. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for a wrong size; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows `blocks` for the call; `*rgba` is replaced. |

## `EncodeBlock(BcKind,const byte*,const BcEncodeOptions&,byte*)` {#asset-encodeblock-bckind-const-byte-star-const-bcencodeoptions-amp-byte-star}

`function asset::EncodeBlock` — defined in `engine/asset/block_compress.h`

```cpp
void EncodeBlock(BcKind kind, const byte* rgba, const BcEncodeOptions& options, byte* block);
```

Encodes one block from 16 RGBA8 texels (64 bytes, row-major) into `BcBlockBytes(kind)` bytes.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `kind` | `Bc1`, `Bc4`, `Bc5` or `Bc7`. |
| Parameter `rgba` | 64 bytes. |
| Parameter `options` | the effort and the BC1 alpha rule. |
| Parameter `block` | receives the block. |
| Aborts | `kind` is not one of the four encodable schemes. |
| Ownership | borrows `rgba` and `block` for the call. |

## `EncodeImage(BcKind,u32,u32,ByteSpan,const BcEncodeOptions&,jobs::Scheduler*,Vector<byte>*)` {#asset-encodeimage-bckind-u32-u32-bytespan-const-bcencodeoptions-amp-jobs-scheduler-star-vector-lt-byte-gt-star}

`function asset::EncodeImage` — defined in `engine/asset/block_compress.h`

```cpp
Status EncodeImage(BcKind kind, u32 width, u32 height, ByteSpan rgba, const BcEncodeOptions& options, jobs::Scheduler* scheduler, Vector<byte>* blocks);
```

Encodes a whole image: tight RGBA8 texels in, rows of tightly packed blocks out. Block rows are spread over `scheduler` when it is not null (the output does not depend on the worker count).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `kind` | `Bc1`, `Bc4`, `Bc5` or `Bc7`. |
| Parameter `width` | 1 to [`asset::kMaxTextureSize`](#asset-kmaxtexturesize). |
| Parameter `height` | 1 to [`asset::kMaxTextureSize`](#asset-kmaxtexturesize). |
| Parameter `rgba` | `width * height * 4` bytes. |
| Parameter `options` | the effort and the BC1 alpha rule. |
| Parameter `scheduler` | the job scheduler to fan out on, or null to encode on the calling thread; when given, the caller must be its participant 0 outside any job. |
| Parameter `blocks` | replaced by the blocks. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for an unsupported scheme or a wrong size; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; the workers write disjoint parts of `*blocks`. |
| Ownership | borrows `rgba` and `scheduler` for the call; `*blocks` is replaced. |

## `BuildClipBlob(const TypeRegistry&,ResourceId,const ClipBuildInput&,Vector<byte>*,u32,f32*,f32*)` {#asset-buildclipblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-clipbuildinput-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-f32-star-f32-star}

`function asset::BuildClipBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/clip.h`

```cpp
Status BuildClipBlob(const TypeRegistry& types, ResourceId id, const ClipBuildInput& input, Vector<byte>* out, u32 target = 0, f32* rotation_degrees = nullptr, f32* translation = nullptr);
```

Builds a clip blob: reduces the keys of every channel (a deterministic greedy split that keeps the error inside the bounds), folds constant channels into the track table, quantizes, measures the error against the source keys at every source key and at 8 points inside each segment, and records the measured bounds in the header.

A clip with a weight track and no joint tracks may have no skeleton (a null id and a joint count of 0); it then has no dependency and binds to no skeleton.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | `Invalid` (no tracks, no events and no weights, a duration that is not finite and above 0, a weight track whose times or values are out of order, outside the duration, not finite or of the wrong count, a joint at or past the joint count or used twice, keys not strictly increasing or outside [0, duration], a value that is not finite, a rotation that is not unit length, events out of order or past the duration, a null skeleton); `LimitExceeded` (a count above the limits, or a measured error above `limits`). |
| Ownership | borrows `input` for the call; `*out` is replaced; `*rotation_degrees` and `*translation`, when not null, receive the measured errors (also on a `LimitExceeded` failure). |

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | the errors of [`asset::BlobReader::Open`](#asset-blobreader-open-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp); `TypeMismatch` for another type; `Corrupt` for a missing or mis-sized section, a count above the limit, a track whose joint is out of range or not above the previous one, a channel that is both constant and keyed or keyed with fewer than 2 keys, a key block that is outside the key section or misaligned, key times that do not increase, a constant that is not finite (or a rotation that is not unit length), events out of order or past the duration, a non-finite header value, or a missing or wrong skeleton dependency. |
| Ownership | borrows `data` and `types` for the call; the view keeps spans into `data`. |

`method asset::ClipView::Sample` — defined in `engine/asset/clip.h`

```cpp
ClipSample Sample(u32 i, f32 time, u32* cursors = nullptr) const;
```

Samples track `i` at `time` (clamped to [0, duration]; a NaN time reads as 0).

`cursors`, when given, points at 3 `u32` (translation, rotation, scale) that remember the key segment found by the last call: forward playback then costs O(1) per channel, and the result is the same as without them (any start value is valid; keep one set per track and clip).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Aborts | `i` is not below `TrackCount()`. |
| Ownership | borrows its arguments for the call. |

## `RegisterCpuLoaders(ResourceManager*)` {#asset-registercpuloaders-resourcemanager-star}

`function asset::RegisterCpuLoaders` — defined in `engine/asset/cpu_loaders.h`

```cpp
void RegisterCpuLoaders(ResourceManager* manager);
```

Registers the loaders of audio clips, scripts, skeletons, clips, meshes, textures, materials, mesh colliders and levels with `manager`. A skinned mesh is published only when its skeleton is loaded and its recorded hash and joint count match, and a clip only when its skeleton is the one it was made for (`DependencyTypeMismatch` otherwise).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `manager` | the manager. |
| Ownership | borrows `manager` for the call only; nothing is retained. |

```cpp
void RegisterSkeletonLoader(ResourceManager* manager);
```

|---|---|
| Parameter `manager` | the manager. |
| Ownership | borrows `manager` for the call only; nothing is retained. |

```cpp
u32 FloatToHalfBits(f32 value);
```

The half float nearest to `value` (ties to even) as its 16 bits, clamped to the largest finite half (65504).

## `CubeFaceDirection(u32,f32,f32)` {#asset-cubefacedirection-u32-f32-f32}

`function asset::CubeFaceDirection` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
Vec3 CubeFaceDirection(u32 face, f32 u, f32 v);
```

The direction a cube face texel looks along (not normalized), in the convention of the three graphics APIs.

The face order is +X, -X, +Y, -Y, +Z, -Z; `u` runs along the face's rows from the left edge (-1) to the right edge (+1) and `v` down its columns from the top edge (-1) to the bottom edge (+1), both at texel centers `2 * (i + 0.5) / n - 1`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `face` | 0 to 5. |
| Parameter `u` | the horizontal face coordinate, -1 to 1. |
| Parameter `v` | the vertical face coordinate, -1 to 1. |
| Aborts | `face` is 6 or more. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |

## `DeterministicAtan2(f32,f32)` {#asset-deterministicatan2-f32-f32}

`function asset::DeterministicAtan2` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
f32 DeterministicAtan2(f32 y, f32 x);
```

The angle in radians of the point `(x, y)` from the positive x axis, -pi to pi, by a fixed polynomial (error under 1e-5 radians); `(0, 0)` gives 0.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |

## `EquirectangularToCubeFaces(ByteSpan,u32,u32,u32,Vector<Vector<byte>>*)` {#asset-equirectangulartocubefaces-bytespan-u32-u32-u32-vector-lt-vector-lt-byte-gt-gt-star}

`function asset::EquirectangularToCubeFaces` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
Status EquirectangularToCubeFaces(ByteSpan rgba16f, u32 width, u32 height, u32 face_size, Vector<Vector<byte>>* faces);
```

Converts an equirectangular image of half-float RGBA texels into the six faces of a cube map, bilinearly sampled.

The image's center is the direction -Z, its columns run toward +X (east, to the right when looking along -Z), its top row is straight up (+Y). Texels must be finite.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `rgba16f` | `width * height * 8` bytes of half-float RGBA, rows tightly packed. |
| Parameter `width` | 2 to 16384 texels across. |
| Parameter `height` | 1 to 16384 texels down. |
| Parameter `face_size` | texels across a face, 1 to [`asset::kMaxCubeFaceSize`](#asset-kmaxcubefacesize). |
| Parameter `faces` | replaced by six faces of `face_size * face_size * 8` bytes each, in the order +X -X +Y -Y +Z -Z. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for a size out of range or bytes of the wrong count; `Corrupt` for an infinite or NaN texel; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | borrows `rgba16f`; `*faces` is replaced. |

## `CubeDirectionToFace(const Vec3&,u32*,f32*,f32*)` {#asset-cubedirectiontoface-const-vec3-amp-u32-star-f32-star-f32-star}

`function asset::CubeDirectionToFace` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
void CubeDirectionToFace(const Vec3& direction, u32* face, f32* u, f32* v);
```

The face and the texel coordinates a direction hits: the inverse of [`asset::CubeFaceDirection`](#asset-cubefacedirection-u32-f32-f32).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `direction` | any non-zero direction. |
| Parameter `face` | set to 0 to 5. |
| Parameter `u` | set to the horizontal face coordinate, -1 to 1. |
| Parameter `v` | set to the vertical face coordinate, -1 to 1. |
| Fails | never. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | the three out parameters are written. |

## `DeterministicSinCos(f32,f32*,f32*)` {#asset-deterministicsincos-f32-f32-star-f32-star}

`function asset::DeterministicSinCos` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
void DeterministicSinCos(f32 radians, f32* sine, f32* cosine);
```

The sine and cosine of `radians`, from fixed polynomials (error under 1e-6 within one turn).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `radians` | 0 to 2 pi. |
| Parameter `sine` | set to the sine. |
| Parameter `cosine` | set to the cosine. |

## `PrefilterEnvironmentChains(u32,u32,Vector<Vector<byte>>*)` {#asset-prefilterenvironmentchains-u32-u32-vector-lt-vector-lt-byte-gt-gt-star}

`function asset::PrefilterEnvironmentChains` — defined in `engine/asset/cube_map.h`

```cpp
Status PrefilterEnvironmentChains(u32 face_size, u32 mips, Vector<Vector<byte>>* chains);
```

Replaces the levels 1 to `mips - 1` of the six half-float face chains of a cube map with its specular chain: level `i` is the box-filtered level `i` convolved with a GGX lobe of roughness `i / (mips - 1)` (alpha is its square), by 32 importance samples in a fixed order, so a shader reads a rougher reflection from a higher level.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `face_size` | texels across a face at level 0 (a power of two or not; levels halve down to 1). |
| Parameter `mips` | the levels stored; at most the full chain. 1 changes nothing. |
| Parameter `chains` | six tight chains of `RGBA16_Float` levels, level 0 first, as the texture minter builds them; the levels from 1 are overwritten. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for a face size of 0, a mip count of 0 or above the full chain, or chains of the wrong number or size. |
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | borrows nothing past the call; `*chains` is modified. |

## `FilesystemBackend` {#asset-filesystembackend}

`class asset::FilesystemBackend` — defined in `engine/asset/filesystem_backend.h`

```cpp
class FilesystemBackend : public ResourceBackend { ... }
```

Serves the blobs under a root directory.

The index (id, type, virtual path, file) is built once, on first use, either from the stored table `resources.table` at the root (a minted `NAMES` blob) or, when there is none, by one walk of the directory that reads each file's 64-byte header (the path is the file's location under the root, the id the blob's own, never an id from a file name). A stored table is a cache that is never trusted over the files: `Stat` and `Open` check the header of the file they find against the id they were asked for. Files that do not start with the blob magic are ignored by the walk; two files that claim one id fail the build of the index. `Watch` rescans and reports what changed (modification stamps and sizes decide, so an unchanged file is not read again).

The walk goes 64 directories deep; a blob below that is not indexed.

A path never leaves the root: the index only holds files found under it, and a symbolic link is followed only when its target is inside the root too (loops fail to resolve and are skipped).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe (one lock around the index). |
| Ownership | owns its index; `types` must outlive the backend. |

### `Create(const io::Path&,const TypeRegistry&,UniquePtr<FilesystemBackend>*,u32)` {#asset-filesystembackend-create-const-io-path-amp-const-typeregistry-amp-uniqueptr-lt-filesystembackend-gt-star-u32}

`method asset::FilesystemBackend::Create` — defined in `engine/asset/filesystem_backend.h`

```cpp
static Status Create(const io::Path& root, const TypeRegistry& types, UniquePtr<FilesystemBackend>* out, u32 target = 0);
```

Makes a backend over `root`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `target` | the target tag of the running program; a blob laid out for another target is refused with `WrongTarget`. |
| Fails | `NotFound` when `root` is not a directory; `Io` when it cannot be examined. |
| Ownership | `*out` receives the backend on success; the caller owns it. |

```cpp
struct GpuMaterial { ... }
```

A material in the material system, with its textures bound by owner and its samplers. The material system holds a share of every texture and sampler the material binds, so none can go while the material uses it; this holder is shared itself: a spawned level's entities name its slot in the material system, so the level holds a share of the holder and a reload of the resource does not destroy the material under them.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns the material (destroyed with the last share). |

## `RegisterGpuFallbacks(ResourceManager*)` {#asset-registergpufallbacks-resourcemanager-star}

`function asset::RegisterGpuFallbacks` — defined in `engine/asset/gpu_loaders.h`

```cpp
Status RegisterGpuFallbacks(ResourceManager* manager);
```

Registers the fallbacks of the GPU types: a unit cube for meshes, a magenta and black checker for textures and a magenta material, made in memory (they are in no mount and never reach a dependency). They stand in for a missing or invalid resource of the type only when `assets.allow_fallbacks` is on, each use logged and counted in [`asset::ResourceStats::fallbacks_used`](#asset-resourcestats-fallbacks-used).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `manager` | the manager; its type registry is the one the blobs are built against. |
| Fails | the failures of the blob builders (not expected). |
| Ownership | borrows `manager` for the call; the blobs are copied into it. |

## `RegisterGpuLoaders(ResourceManager*,GpuAssetContext*)` {#asset-registergpuloaders-resourcemanager-star-gpuassetcontext-star}

`function asset::RegisterGpuLoaders` — defined in `engine/asset/gpu_loaders.h`

```cpp
void RegisterGpuLoaders(ResourceManager* manager, GpuAssetContext* context);
```

Registers the loaders of meshes ([`asset::GpuMeshAsset`](#asset-gpumeshasset), built from the blob's own bytes, nothing copied; a skinned mesh is checked against its skeleton and needs a skinned material), textures ([`asset::GpuTextureAsset`](#asset-gputextureasset)) and materials ([`asset::GpuMaterialAsset`](#asset-gpumaterialasset), textures bound by owner), replacing the CPU loaders of those types.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `manager` | the manager. |
| Parameter `context` | the device, registry and material system; it must stay alive and unchanged until the manager is destroyed. |
| Ownership | borrows both; nothing is copied. |

## `BuildHeightMapBlob(const TypeRegistry&,ResourceId,const HeightMapHeader&,ByteSpan,Vector<byte>*,u32)` {#asset-buildheightmapblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-heightmapheader-amp-bytespan-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32}

| Ownership | `samples` is read during the call; `out` receives a copy. |

## `BuildHullGeometry(Span<const Array<f32,3>>,HullGeometry*)` {#asset-buildhullgeometry-span-lt-const-array-lt-f32-3-gt-gt-hullgeometry-star}

`function asset::BuildHullGeometry` — defined in `engine/asset/hull.h`

```cpp
Status BuildHullGeometry(Span<const Array<f32, 3>> points, HullGeometry* out);
```

Computes the convex hull of a point cloud, deterministically.

Points closer together than 1e-6 of the cloud's size are welded (the first stays); the hull is built incrementally in double precision; coplanar triangles merge into one polygon face; the vertices that remain are numbered in input order. The volume, centroid and inertia are computed from the faces.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `points` | 4 to `kMaxHullInputPoints` points, finite. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the hull. |
| Returns | `Invalid` for fewer than four points, a non-finite point, or points that are all on a line or a plane (thinner than 1e-5 of their size); `LimitExceeded` for too many input points, more than `kMaxHullVertices` vertices, `kMaxHullFaces` faces or `kMaxHullFaceVertices` vertices on a face. |
| Fails | as the return says. |
| Ownership | `*out` is replaced; nothing is borrowed after the call. |

## `BuildHullBlob(const TypeRegistry&,ResourceId,const HullGeometry&,Vector<byte>*,u32)` {#asset-buildhullblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-hullgeometry-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32}

| Ownership | `out` receives the bytes; nothing is borrowed after the call. |

## `StaticCollider` {#asset-staticcollider}

`struct asset::StaticCollider` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
struct StaticCollider { ... }
```

The stored collision of a level entity: one static collider at the entity, sized in the entity's own space. The sizes are unscaled: whoever makes the physics body applies the entity's world scale when the body is made (the C API's `level_spawn`). A mesh shape names an `MCOL` resource, so the level's closure (its reference table) includes it.

|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

## `LevelNameValid(StringView)` {#asset-levelnamevalid-stringview}

`function asset::LevelNameValid` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
bool LevelNameValid(StringView name);
```

Returns true when `name` is a valid entity name: 1 to [`asset::kMaxLevelNameBytes`](#asset-kmaxlevelnamebytes) characters from letters, digits and `_ . : / -`, and not the word `key` (the text form uses `#` for comments and `@` for literal ids and keys, so neither is a name character).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | returns false as described. |

## `LevelEntityKey(ResourceId,StringView)` {#asset-levelentitykey-resourceid-stringview}

`function asset::LevelEntityKey` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
storage::EntityKey LevelEntityKey(ResourceId level, StringView name);
```

The key of the entity called `name` in the level `level`: the first 16 bytes of the content hash of the level id (8 bytes, little-endian) followed by the name, so the same text always gives the same keys and two levels may use one name.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `level` | the level's own id. |
| Parameter `name` | a valid name. |
| Returns | a non-null key. |
| Fails | never. |

## `ReadLevelSummary(ByteSpan,const TypeRegistry&,LevelSummary*)` {#asset-readlevelsummary-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-levelsummary-star}

`function asset::ReadLevelSummary` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
Status ReadLevelSummary(ByteSpan bytes, const TypeRegistry& types, LevelSummary* out);
```

Reads the header and the dependency list of a level blob, checking only the container and the header's own consistency (the sizes of the sections against the counts, the entity table against the containers' section); the records are not read. The loader of the resource manager uses it so that loading a level copies nothing; spawning the level ([`asset::LoadLevelBlob`](#asset-loadlevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-ecs-world-amp-storage-keyindex-amp-leveldata-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp)) validates the records.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `bytes` | the blob. |
| Parameter `types` | the registry of resource types. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the summary. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` for an inconsistent header or section; `UnsupportedVersion` (the message names `asset_mint remint`) for another container or type version; `TypeMismatch` for a blob of another type; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows `bytes` for the call; `*out` is replaced. |

## `BuildLevelBlob(const TypeRegistry&,const storage::ComponentRegistry&,ResourceId,const LevelData&,Vector<byte>*,u32,const storage::CodecLimits&)` {#asset-buildlevelblob-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-resourceid-const-leveldata-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-const-storage-codeclimits-amp}

`function asset::BuildLevelBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
Status BuildLevelBlob(const TypeRegistry& types, const storage::ComponentRegistry& registry, ResourceId id, const LevelData& data, Vector<byte>* out, u32 target = 0, const storage::CodecLimits& limits = storage::CodecLimits());
```

Builds a level blob for `id`: validates every entity container against `registry`, finds the references and checks them against `data.resources`, and writes the sections.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `types` | the registry of resource types. |
| Parameter `registry` | the components; it must hold the builtin components (`entity_key`, `parent_link`). |
| Parameter `id` | the level's id (never null). |
| Parameter `data` | the entities and the resources; `references` is ignored and recomputed. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the blob. |
| Parameter `target` | the target tag recorded in the header. |
| Parameter `limits` | the bounds the records are held to. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for a null id, entities out of key order or with a null key, a container that does not parse, a record of an unknown, derived, skipped or structural component (`entity_key`), a component twice in one entity, a null id in a field that is not nullable, a reference whose resource is not listed or has another type than the field declares, a listed resource nothing names, a resource listed twice or out of order, more than [`asset::kMaxLevelEntities`](#asset-kmaxlevelentities) entities; `UnsupportedVersion` for a record not at its registered version; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows its arguments for the call; `*out` is replaced. |

## `ParseLevelBlob(ByteSpan,const TypeRegistry&,const storage::ComponentRegistry&,LevelData*,ResourceId*,const storage::CodecLimits&)` {#asset-parselevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-leveldata-star-resourceid-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp}

`function asset::ParseLevelBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
Status ParseLevelBlob(ByteSpan bytes, const TypeRegistry& types, const storage::ComponentRegistry& registry, LevelData* out, ResourceId* id_out = nullptr, const storage::CodecLimits& limits = storage::CodecLimits());
```

Reads a level blob into a description, with every check [`asset::BuildLevelBlob`](#asset-buildlevelblob-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-resourceid-const-leveldata-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32-const-storage-codeclimits-amp) makes plus the blob's own layout: the table against the sections, the reference table against the references found in the records, and the dependency list against both.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `bytes` | the blob. |
| Parameter `types` | the registry of resource types. |
| Parameter `registry` | the components. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the description. |
| Parameter `id_out` | receives the blob's own id when not null. |
| Parameter `limits` | the bounds the records are held to. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` for any inconsistency; `UnsupportedVersion` (the message names `asset_mint remint`) for another container, type or component version; `TypeMismatch` for a blob of another type; `NotFound` for a record of a component the registry does not know; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows `bytes` for the call; `*out` is replaced. |

## `LoadLevelBlob(ByteSpan,const TypeRegistry&,const storage::ComponentRegistry&,ecs::World&,storage::KeyIndex&,LevelData*,const storage::CodecLimits&)` {#asset-loadlevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-ecs-world-amp-storage-keyindex-amp-leveldata-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp}

`function asset::LoadLevelBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/level.h`

```cpp
Status LoadLevelBlob(ByteSpan bytes, const TypeRegistry& types, const storage::ComponentRegistry& registry, ecs::World& world, storage::KeyIndex& index, LevelData* data = nullptr, const storage::CodecLimits& limits = storage::CodecLimits());
```

Creates the level's entities in `world`: parses the blob, then loads its records the way `storage::WorldStorage` does, so the world is untouched when the blob or any record is bad. References are left as the ids the components hold; the resource manager resolves them. Derived state (transforms, bounds) is not stored: run the systems once afterwards.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `bytes` | the blob. |
| Parameter `types` | the registry of resource types. |
| Parameter `registry` | the components. |
| Parameter `world` | receives the entities. |
| Parameter `index` | the keys of `world`; updated. |
| Parameter `data` | receives the parsed description (the resources and references the manager needs) when not null. |
| Parameter `limits` | the bounds the records are held to. |
| Fails | the errors of [`asset::ParseLevelBlob`](#asset-parselevelblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-const-storage-componentregistry-amp-leveldata-star-resourceid-star-const-storage-codeclimits-amp); `Exists` when an entity key is in the world already; the errors of `storage::WorldStorage::Load` (`Corrupt` for a record that does not decode, `MissingReference` for a parent that is not in the level); on any failure the world is as it was. |
| Ownership | borrows its arguments for the call; `*data` is replaced. |

```cpp

```

Frees every idle buffer; the buffers still handed out must have been released.

### `Acquire(usize,LoadBuffer*)` {#asset-loadbufferpool-acquire-usize-loadbuffer-star}

`method asset::LoadBufferPool::Acquire` — defined in `engine/asset/load_buffer_pool.h`

```cpp
Status Acquire(usize size, LoadBuffer* out);
```

Hands out a buffer of at least `size` bytes, reusing an idle one of its class when there is one.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | `LimitExceeded` when the buffer would take the pool past its budget even after dropping every idle buffer; `TooLarge` for a size of `kMaxBlobBytes` or more; `Invalid` for a size of 0; `Other` when memory ran out. |
| Ownership | `*out` receives the buffer on success; the pool must outlive it. |

## `BuildMaterialBlob(const TypeRegistry&,ResourceId,const MaterialAssetData&,Vector<byte>*,u32)` {#asset-buildmaterialblob-const-typeregistry-amp-resourceid-const-materialassetdata-amp-vector-lt-byte-gt-star-u32}

`function asset::BuildMaterialBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/material.h`

```cpp
Status BuildMaterialBlob(const TypeRegistry& types, ResourceId id, const MaterialAssetData& data, Vector<byte>* out, u32 target = 0);
```

Builds a material blob for `id`: validates `data`, writes the sections and declares each texture as a dependency.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `types` | the registry (the texture type's version goes into the dependency records). |
| Parameter `id` | the material's id (never null). |
| Parameter `data` | the description. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the blob. |
| Parameter `target` | the target tag recorded in the header. |
| Fails | `Invalid` for a null id, an empty or unterminated name, a repeated name, counts or sizes above the limits, a parameter outside the block, overlapping parameters, a null or repeated texture id, a repeated slot, a sampler outside its ranges or a state value outside its enum; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows `data` for the call; `*out` is replaced. |

## `LoadMaterialBlob(ByteSpan,const TypeRegistry&,MaterialAssetData*,ResourceId*)` {#asset-loadmaterialblob-bytespan-const-typeregistry-amp-materialassetdata-star-resourceid-star}

`function asset::LoadMaterialBlob` — defined in `engine/asset/material.h`

```cpp
Status LoadMaterialBlob(ByteSpan bytes, const TypeRegistry& types, MaterialAssetData* out, ResourceId* id_out = nullptr);
```

Reads a material blob into a description. Everything is validated: the sections' sizes against the header, every name, the parameters against the block, the enums and ranges, and the dependency list against the texture bindings.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `bytes` | the blob. |
| Parameter `types` | the registry. |
| Parameter `out` | replaced by the description. |
| Parameter `id_out` | receives the blob's own id when not null. |
| Fails | `Corrupt` for any inconsistency; `UnsupportedVersion` (the message names `asset_mint remint`) for another container or type version; `TypeMismatch` for a blob of another type; `LimitExceeded` when memory runs out. |
| Ownership | borrows `bytes` for the call; `*out` is replaced. |

### `release` {#asset-memoryaddoptions-release}

`variable asset::MemoryAddOptions::release` — defined in `engine/asset/memory_backend.h`

```cpp
void (*release)(void* user, ByteSpan bytes);
```

Lends the bytes instead of copying them: the backend reads the host's memory in place, which must stay valid and unchanged until `release` runs, and calls `release(release_user, bytes)` exactly once, when the last holder of the resource (the backend, or a reader of a replaced or removed one) lets go, on the thread that lets go. A call that fails never calls it (the host keeps the bytes). Null copies the bytes.

`variable asset::MemoryAddOptions::release_user` — defined in `engine/asset/memory_backend.h`

```cpp

```

## `HostMemoryBackend` {#asset-hostmemorybackend}

`class asset::HostMemoryBackend` — defined in `engine/asset/memory_backend.h`

```cpp
class HostMemoryBackend : public ResourceBackend { ... }
```

Resources provided by the host.

The bytes of every resource are a complete blob: `Add` checks the container, that the blob's own id is the id the host passed, that the content hash verifies and, when the host passes one, that it equals the host's hash. Bytes are copied into memory the backend owns. A replace never tears a read: an open reader keeps the bytes it opened. Every add, replace and remove leaves a `Watch` record. The bytes of all resources count against the budget (`assets.memory_backend_bytes`); an add past it returns `LimitExceeded` (back-pressure, never eviction: the host owns the content).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe; adds, replaces and removes may run concurrently with reads. |
| Ownership | owns its bytes. Readers must not outlive the backend object, but the bytes they read outlive a replacement. |

### `HostMemoryBackend(const TypeRegistry&,u64)` {#asset-hostmemorybackend-hostmemorybackend-const-typeregistry-amp-u64}

`constructor asset::HostMemoryBackend::HostMemoryBackend` — defined in `engine/asset/memory_backend.h`

```cpp
HostMemoryBackend(const TypeRegistry& types, u64 budget_bytes);
```

Makes a backend that checks blobs against `types` (which must outlive it) and holds at most `budget_bytes`.

`destructor asset::HostMemoryBackend::~HostMemoryBackend` — defined in `engine/asset/memory_backend.h`

```cpp
~HostMemoryBackend() override;
```

Frees the resources it holds (a reader keeps the bytes it opened).
